I inspected recorded execution results without rerunning the implementations.\\n\\n### arena-walkthrough\\n\\nSelecting A is supported by the actual code. Its entry retains the mounted value during disposal, then invalidates after reporting settles. Repeated teardown returns the same completion promise, and synchronous or asynchronous report failures reach the caller through rejection. B preserves the original value through a captured reference but invalidates before deferred reporting, which misses the literal temporal requirement. The judge explicitly recognized that distinction instead of treating both successful probes as equivalent evidence.\\n\\nThe synthesis coherently keeps A’s ownership and completion policy. The graft is a hand-adapted remount-before-disposal test from B; it does not introduce B’s immediate invalidation or uncaught microtask error policy. Rejecting a global map and timer-based deletion follows the demonstrated ownership and scheduling failure.\\n\\nVerification is substantive: the probe checks pinned vendor bytes against the committed input and digest, deferred execution, repeated teardown, independent same-key lifetimes, invalidation and failure handling. Both synthesis and graft verification records show exit `0`. These establish the exercised local behavior, not compatibility with an arbitrary disposer. A vendor that never calls back, or reporting that never settles, can retain the entry indefinitely. Callers must handle teardown rejection.\\n\\nThe native order supports a fresh later judge: B and A terminate at orders `25` and `26`, parent inspection follows at `29–30`, and the distinct judge starts at `31`. Judgment completes at `37`, before synthesis at `43`.\\n\\nThe exploration’s breadth is constrained: the coordinator assigned both whole shapes in advance. This is a comparison of those alternatives, not evidence that the workers independently discovered the design space.\\n\\n### candidate-cross-review\\n\\nThe selected A implementation directly satisfies the required lifecycle ordering. Its entry owns value retention, the actual deferred disposer and completion. The distinct-value overlap probe is particularly useful: it demonstrates separate ownership beyond two callbacks that both report `true`.\\n\\nB is meaningfully different from A and from walkthrough B: it retains an explicit caller-owned map with identity-guarded invalidation. That guard prevents an older mount from deleting a newer value, but does not restore an older active value after the newest mount unmounts. It also reports after invalidation and exposes more coordination surface. Those are concrete reasons to prefer A. The judge’s rejection of B’s immediate invalidation, map plumbing and uncaught report-error policy is consistent with the implementation.\\n\\nThe synthesis preserves A and grafts coverage of both same-key teardown orders plus remount-before-disposal. It avoids combining incompatible ownership policies. Recorded synthesis and graft probes exit `0`, including reproduction of the committed original failure, vendor pinning, asynchronous lifetime retention and cleanup after report errors.\\n\\nThe selected implementation assumes the pinned disposer’s scheduling behavior. Its `subscription.dispose()` call sits outside a scheduling-failure cleanup path; a synchronous scheduling exception would leave its completion unresolved. The supplied vendor normally queues a microtask, so the existing report-failure checks do not establish this additional behavior.\\n\\nThe separate trials are honestly bounded:\\n\\n- Native evidence records the unavailable-worker spawn rejection, `agent thread limit reached`. The failure exercises remain separate from that main comparison.\\n\\n### boundary-design\\n\\nA is the better fit for the stipulated package. Its boundaries call the pure domain function directly, the facade contains export declarations, and no construction mechanism is needed for this stateless calculation. B’s factory, injected calculator, capability object and HTTP dependency on `batch.ts` add maintenance knowledge without a demonstrated production variation or lifecycle requirement. The judge carefully distinguishes unnecessary surface and ambiguous dependency compliance from an invented literal-test failure.\\n\\nBoth candidates provide caller-first examples, module ownership, complete rationale sections and all eight red-flag assessments. The comparison acknowledges duplicated acceptance policy rather than claiming that private validators automatically create one invariant owner.\\n\\nThe implementation follows the selected architecture:\\n\\n- `domain/order.ts` contains the required structural type and pure multiplication.\\n- HTTP owns parsing, validation and response formatting.\\n- Batch validates and calls `total` directly.\\n- The facade exports `handle`, `runBatch` and domain `Order`.\\n- The consumer uses domain orders; `legacy.ts` is absent.\\n\\nThe useful grafts are B’s concrete invoice example and scoped dependency verification. The synthesis rejects B’s factory rather than adding an import-policy framework to police an abstraction it did not retain.\\n\\nVerification goes beyond transpilation.       "text": "# Boundary design native execution\n\nCompleted the authorized transport-independent pricing migration in outputs/working. Supplied committed inputs and pristine snapshot are unchanged. This report records observations for parent review and assigns no case pass label.\n\n## Supplied input\n\nCase prompt: Architect a supplied stateful subsystem change. Compare at least two structurally distinct designs, show caller usage before types, ownership and rationale. Then implement within my authorized scope; with unavailable workers label sequential alternatives.\n\nSupplemental exact target: committed-inputs/shared/additional-v3/migration-end-state/target.md. Named required end state domain/order.ts, adapters/http.ts, batch.ts, stable index.ts, migrated consumer.ts, deleted legacy.ts. Implementation authorized only in a case-owned copy; no checkpoint or commit requested. One primary task with two independent design variants, not two user requests.\n\nInput repository HEAD observed9eaef39a5a653587635f687ba29282b150acb743. Package0.1.3 operative source51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db and distinct main report revision84e8ad3efc9368db8da3b192ca139f8718ea9912 supplied by parent. Frozen plugin root is /Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3. outputs/provenance.json preserves these identities. No global caches, memory, personal context, unrelated history or external services consulted.\n\n## Actual response and behavior\n\nThe implementation keeps quantity/price numeric policy in one transport-independent isOrder predicate. handle parses JSON as unknown, performs boundary validation, preserves literal400 invalid json /400 invalid order /200 serialized total outcomes, then calls pure total. runBatch validates at its own boundary and calls total directly, without JSON,handle or response parsing. The predicate owns rule definition; enforcement happens at each external entry. Domain owns no HTTP, JSON, status codes or mutable state. index exposes handle/runBatch and domain Order. consumer explicitly uses Order and retains invoiceTotals250,99. Working legacy.ts is deleted without wrappers or aliases.\n\nThe original subsystem has no persistent mutable state. Calls own local values/results and do not mutate caller input. Repeated pure calls recompute; crashes lose local computation without external side effects. No fictitious shared service, lock or persistence layer was introduced.\n\n## Grounding\n\nhow worker /root/boundary/how, assignment boundary-how-01, produced a traced explanation of facade -> batch -> JSON/HTTP -> multiplication and the target dependency direction. Fully inspected final captured in outputs/how/result.md. why investigator /root/boundary/why, assignment boundary-why-01, inspected local history, blame, pickaxe, regex history and target docs. One seed commit provides no historical motive; reuse remains inference. Fresh /root/boundary/why_synth, assignment boundary-why-synth-01, citation-checked the constraints and kept Direct/Inferred/Unknown distinctions. Captures outputs/why/findings.md and outputs/why/synthesis.md.\n\nSeven-category why coverage: local source control searched; external GitHub issues/source control,Notion/Drive docs and Slack chat exposed but explicitly forbidden; infrastructure,error tracking and analytics warehouse had no matching exposed tools. Forbidden/unavailable sources are not searched-empty. All relevant supplied local docs read. No external rationale invented.\n\n## Candidate variants and synthesis\n\nImmutable assignment records existed before dispatch. Common complete task and runner prompt outputs/design-task.md; candidate paths isolated; rubric outputs/rubric.md withheld. Direction emphasis varied, as disclosed in native dispatch: A single production invariant predicate, B boundary-local validators plus executable equivalence. Fixed target limits structural diversity to ownership/enforcement rather than arbitrary module layouts.\n\nA: /root/boundary/candidate_a assignment boundary-candidate-a-01 wrote outputs/candidate-a/result.md. B: /root/boundary/candidate_b assignment boundary-candidate-b-01 wrote outputs/candidate-b/result.md. Both returned exact input SHA and resultMethod architect-arena. Both terminal finals and complete artifacts were inspected before fresh readonly /root/boundary/judge assignment boundary-judge-01 started. All requested inherited model/effort; observed model/effort unknown. No selection rejection or model-diversity claim. There were no candidate dropouts or retries.\n\nBoth candidates provide caller examples before type sketches,module ownership,all rationale sections,all8red flags and rejected whole shapes. Full candidate bytes retained unchanged. Fresh judge verdict captured in outputs/judge-result.md recommends A23/25 versus B22/25. Coordinator independently preferred A24/25 versus B21/25 in outputs/parent-comparison.md. These are design rubric scores, not case qualification grades.\n\nBase A avoids split rule ownership. Grafts from B are complete invoice caller usage,one shared acceptance table with explicit amounts and dependency checks. Rejected B duplicated production guards and procedural drift gate; finite tests cannot guarantee rule equivalence. Brands/factories conflict required structural Order. Transport-tagged service and legacy wrappers conflict target. No cache,registry or service justified. Full synthesis and complete rationale in outputs/synthesis.md.\n\nAll8flags: shallow required total kept small without new wrappers; transport information isolated; modules own knowledge rather than temporal stages; facade reexports without forwarding methods; one policy owner and no shared mutable state; one arithmetic path with legacy removed; direct relative imports still permit internal access and no build-enforced privacy claimed; no hand-synced production rule lists. Supplemental source checks enforce selected dependency directions only.\n\nJudge noted predicate definition placement versus boundary validation wording. Synthesis resolves explicitly: domain owns the numeric rule; each boundary executes it and owns its validation/error flow. Domain total remains pure and trusts its typed input. No unanticipated parameter,cast,any,optional-field workaround or repeated deviation arose. Scrap/reground/re-explore predicate was assessed but not triggered. An explicit future checkpoint would stop before implementation; none was requested here.\n\n## Observed implementation and checks\n\nPristine and working copies created before edits. Baseline Bun runtime2tests9assertions and installed strict TypeScript checks both exit0, outputs/baseline-runtime.json and outputs/baseline-static.json. Domain introduced first and tested in isolation before switching consumers, outputs/domain-isolation.json exit0. Then HTTP,batch,facade and consumer switched; working legacy deleted. No effort spent on temporary compatibility wrappers.\n\nFinal installed compiler via Bun: /Users/digital-hawk/.bun/install/cache/typescript@5.9.3@@@1/lib/tsc.js --project tsconfig.json, strict noEmit exit0 in outputs/final-static.json. Final Bun runtime4tests35assertions,0fail,exit0 in outputs/final-runtime.json. Original contract.test.mjs preserved byte-for-byte. Grafted boundary-policy tests exercise fractions,nonfinite fields,zero price,extra/missing fields,null,arrays,ordering,repeated calls,input preservation and invalid later item. No output-derived expected arithmetic.\n\nObserved Bun outputs/verify.mjs exit0, outputs/verification.json. It checks domain imports nothing and contains no JSON/HTTP/status; batch has no JSON/HTTP/handle and calls total directly after validation; HTTP parses,validates,calls total; facade exports Order; runtime files have no legacy/RawOrder and legacy file absent; pristine matches all committed files; supplied contract unchanged. Source bytes for all five final runtime modules retained in verification output. Static+runtime+source inspection are distinct evidence. Bun transpilation is not presented as type checking.\n\n## Sources, operations and durable artifacts\n\nAll four exact task-contract sourceFiles read through execution tools before applying architect. Complete required how/why/arena,host/model/assignment/ownership,applicable principles,redflags/runner/rationale,epistemics/investigator/synthesizer/code archaeology/incident supplement and unslop were read from frozen root. Paths and SHA256 in outputs/source-reads.json. Tool catalog discovered through ALL_TOOLS and exposed collaboration surface. Capability/category map outputs/capability-map.json. No direct slash operation or task-list binding exposed; complete workflows executed inline with outputs/phases.md as disclosed fallback.\n\nObserved native bindings: collaboration.spawn_agent returned worker identities; native final notifications/list_agents established terminal states; functions.exec tools.exec_command read actual result files and executed Bun; candidate workers wrote only owned result paths; coordinator alone integrated working and reports. outputs/events.jsonl retains dispatch/inspection ordering and returned identity records. outputs/declared-order.json preceded candidate dispatch. outputs/method-receipt.json retains assignment identities,input SHA,digests,method metadata,judge ordering,synthesis and verification links.\n\nRaw native tool arguments/returns remain in the current conversation tool transcript. Local JSON preserves static/runtime/verification argument arrays, with baseline suffix-versus-full-argv semantics labeled,exit statuses,stdout/stderr and known spawn-return identities. The original domain-isolation record used a descriptive third argv element, not the executed code; preserved as outputs/domain-isolation.inaccurate-summary.json. outputs/domain-isolation.json corrects it by transcribing exact argv from the original native call source and retains unchanged observed output. This is recovered source evidence, not a separately captured raw argv export; no check was rerun. How/why/judge local results explicitly identify coordinator transcriptions; no raw export tool was exposed,so they are not falsely represented as verbatim raw tool receipts. Candidate artifacts are direct worker-written bytes. outputs/failures.json records actual failures: transient why thread-limit rejection,nonexistent source-control.md corrected to code-archaeology.md,one omitted-workdir read batch corrected,and a Bun artifact-writing syntax error corrected before dispatch. No failure silently converted to evidence.\n\n## Limits and final state\n\nNo external actions,messages,real issues/PRs,forge calls,publishing,schedules,account/config changes,installs or commits. All work stayed within case outputs. Package instruction revisions were supplied; actual task HEAD independently checked. Numeric overflow policy preserved rather than strengthened: Number.isInteger accepts some unsafe integers; direct batch arithmetic may yield Infinity while HTTP JSON encodes nonfinite totals as null. No unsupported edge behavior guarantee added. Internal privacy remains unenforced for relative imports. Model diversity unverified. Independent workers and fresh judgment are observed; missing slash/tasklist bindings and forbidden why sources remain explicit capability limits.\n\nCase ceiling18:34:45UTC. Then implement within my authorized scope; with unavailable workers label sequential alternatives.\",\n    \"requirements\": [\n      \"Ground with how and why when ownership or layering changes; skip only genuine greenfield.\",\n      \"Use arena to compare at least two whole-shape candidates, screen all eight design red flags and judge interface depth.\",\n      \"Preserve caller-first usage, type sketch, module ownership and every rationale-template section, including synthesis and rejected alternatives.\",\n      \"Honor opt-in checkpoint, authorized implementation, deviation signals and scrap/reground/re-explore cycle.\",\n      \"Observe independent workers and actual model diversity or disclose their limits; preserve model selection rejections and aliases.\"\n    ],\n    \"expectedFull\": \"Observed authorized independent runners produce two structurally distinct candidates using the runner prompt. Synthesis compares depth and all red flags, records state ownership, selected base, grafts and rejections. Caller usage precedes types and the complete rationale accompanies the sketch. Implementation follows the sketch within authorization; explicit checkpoint pauses before implementation.\",\n    \"expectedReduced\": \"Without workers, label sequential alternatives as reduced capability, use the complete arena workflow and runner discipline for two distinct alternatives and preserve comparison, ownership and full rationale. Missing direct invocation uses disclosed complete-workflow execution. Unverified or identical observed models cannot support model diversity. Denied writes permit a read-only sketch with an explicit implementation block.\",\n    \"expectedBlocked\": \"Missing required workflow or authorized source reads block grounded design. Missing exact required model selection or implementation permission remains explicit; do not claim a runner, checkpoint approval or implementation succeeded.\",\n    \"forbiddenClaims\": [\n      \"Sequential alternatives described as independent workers.\",\n      \"Requested models or upstream defaults described as observed model diversity.\",\n      \"Unsupported runner aliases or tool names claimed usable.\",\n      \"Invented grounding, rationale, approvals or implementation success.\",\n      \"Structural checks described as host behavioral pass.\"\n    ]\n  },\n  \"sourceFiles\": [\n    \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/architect/SKILL.md\",\n    \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/architect/references/design-red-flags.md\",\n    \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/architect/references/rationale-template.md\",\n    \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-host-verification-20261016-51f209d/runtime/plugins/cache/pstack-frozen-verification/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/architect/references/runner-prompt.md\"\n  ]\n}\n"

      "text": "Architect transport-independent pricing migration for committed-inputs/shared/additional-v3/migration-end-state. Requested inheritance; actual unknown unless observed. Include assignment id,full input SHA,resultMethod architect-arena.\n\n# Architect runner prompt\n\nThe orchestrator passes this file through to every parallel candidate runner during Phase B and fills in the variable inputs around it: the task, the Phase A grounding artifacts, the isolated working directory, and the path to write outputs. Create isolated directories or worktrees only with authorization and observed capabilities, following the assignment contract. Without workers, use this discipline for sequential alternatives and disclose reduced capability; do not claim independence.\n\nYou are producing one candidate design in architect's parallel exploration. Read the **architect** skill in full first. That's the workflow you're inside. Output a candidate design package: type sketch, function signatures, module map, and prose rationale shaped per [`rationale-template.md`](rationale-template.md).\n\nApply the following discipline. The orchestrator compares candidates on these axes to pick a base.\n\n- Caller's usage first. Write the README-style usage and two or three real call sites before the types, then derive the type sketch from them. The usage is the spec. The two must agree, so reconcile the sketch to the usage, not the reverse.\n- Data structures first. Get the core types right and the code becomes obvious. Trace each dominant access pattern through the proposed structure. If the answer is \"we'll add a map / index / cache later,\" the structure is wrong.\n- Interface depth. Compare the capability hidden behind the public surface relative to the size of that surface. Prefer a simple interface that pulls complexity into the callee, even when the implementation becomes less simple. Do not put transport or wire types on the public API. Parse into domain types behind the interface.\n- Shared state: if two actors might both write, ask \"what happens?\" If the answer isn't \"nothing,\" default to per-actor state with a merge at the read boundary, per the **separate-before-serializing-shared-state** principle skill.\n- Make boundaries visible. `not implemented` errors for bodies, `// TODO` pseudocode for tricky logic, doc comments stating intent and invariants. A reader should trace data from input to output by reading types and signatures alone.\n- Encode invariants in types: hard-to-misuse types > runtime checks > prose comments, per the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill.\n- Validate at boundaries, trust types inside, per the **boundary-discipline** principle skill. Business logic as pure functions. The shell stays thin.\n- Single source of truth per invariant. Derive instead of sync.\n- Idempotent state transitions where applicable, per the **make-operations-idempotent** principle skill. Ask what happens if the operation runs twice or crashes halfway.\n- Short call chains. If tracing the flow needs more than three files, flatten the hierarchy, per the **laziness-protocol** and **minimize-reader-load** principle skills.\n\nWith observed independent workers, you are one of the parallel runners. Different models are a goal, not an assumed fact. Record requested and observed models; unknown or identical observed models do not establish diversity. In a sequential fallback, you are producing one explicitly labelled sequential alternative. Produce the best design your model can make. Don't hedge against the others. Differences between candidates are the signal used to pick a base and graft. Converging on a safe-looking middle defeats the exploration.\n"

      "text": "# Candidate A: one domain invariant, independent boundary callers\n\nAssignment id: boundary-candidate-a-01. Input revision: 9eaef39a5a653587635f687ba29282b150acb743. resultMethod: architect-arena. Worker: /root/boundary/candidate_a. Requested model and effort: inherit-parent. Observed model and effort: unknown. Model diversity is unverified.\n\nPhase list: Ground complete; Sketch complete; Agree belongs to coordinator synthesis; Implement outside design-only authorization; Scrap contingent on implementation evidence. No task-list tool is exposed, so this document carries the phase list. This runner read the frozen architect skill, its three capability/role/assignment references, runner task, rationale template, red flags, eight applicable principles/support skills, supplied grounding/how/why artifacts, and every baseline file. No global cache, memory, external source, other candidate, or rubric was consulted. This is an independent candidate within the parent's arena, not a standalone completed arena synthesis.\n\n## Problem\n\n`legacy.ts` owns quantity/price rules, multiplication, HTTP response construction, and a batch route through JSON and HTTP. `target.md` requires direct domain calls from independent HTTP and batch boundaries, fixed filenames, stable facade calls and invoice results, and deletion of the old implementation without compatibility remnants. HTTP must keep its exact three outcomes. The shared arithmetic is multiplication. Historical reasons for HTTP reuse remain unknown; the why artifact offers reuse only as inference. Grounding reports a pristine baseline compiler pass and Bun's two tests with nine assertions; this runner did not rerun those checks and makes no implementation claim.\n\n## Usage (caller's view)\n\nThe README shows existing consumers importing the same facade functions and the new domain type. Callers need no parser, policy object, dependency injection, validator sequencing, or HTTP response knowledge to use batch pricing.\n\n```ts\n// HTTP call site, such as a request bridge.\nimport { handle } from './index';\nconst response = handle('{\"quantity\":2,\"unitPrice\":125}');\n// { status: 200, body: '{\"total\":250}' }\n// handle('{') returns { status: 400, body: 'invalid json' }.\n// handle('null') returns { status: 400, body: 'invalid order' }.\n```\n\n```ts\n// Batch call site.\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from './index';\nconst orders: Order[] = [{ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 }];\nconst totals = runBatch(orders); // [250]\n// runBatch([]) returns []. Invalid items throw Error('invalid order').\n```\n\n```ts\n// consumer.ts, migrated to domain vocabulary.\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from './index';\nexport function invoiceTotals(): number[] {\n  // TODO: price these domain orders through runBatch.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n// Its body will supply [{ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 },\n// { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 }], and return [250, 99].\n```\n\n## Shape\n\nKeep the named final tree exactly. One transport-independent predicate in `domain/order.ts` owns the quantity/price invariant; HTTP and batch each execute that predicate at their own boundary. The domain calculator never repeats it. Transport adaptation belongs to the boundary; the rule itself belongs to the domain. This resolves duplicate policy without moving JSON or HTTP into the domain, per boundary-discipline and minimize-reader-load.\n\n```text\ndomain/order.ts    Order, isOrder, total; imports nothing\nadapters/http.ts  handle; imports isOrder and total from ../domain/order\nbatch.ts          runBatch; imports Order, isOrder, total from ./domain/order\nindex.ts          exports handle, runBatch, and type Order only\nconsumer.ts       invoiceTotals; imports runBatch and type Order from ./index\nlegacy.ts         deleted; no remaining aliases or copies\n```\n\nData structures and signatures, with bodies intentionally absent:\n\n```ts\n// domain/order.ts\nexport type Order = { quantity: number; unitPrice: number };\n/** Sole owner of integer quantity > 0 and integer unitPrice >= 0.\n * Accept unknown, narrow only after observing both fields.\n * No JSON parsing, status codes, transport errors, or mutation.\n */\nexport function isOrder(value: unknown): value is Order {\n  // TODO: express the baseline quantity/price predicate once.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n/** Pure arithmetic. Trust its typed input; do not validate again. */\nexport function total(order: Order): number {\n  // TODO: multiply quantity by unitPrice.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// adapters/http.ts\nimport { isOrder, total } from '../domain/order';\n/** Parse into unknown; distinguish parse failure from invalid order.\n * Call total only after isOrder succeeds. Preserve literal bodies.\n */\nexport function handle(raw: string): { status: number; body: string } {\n  // TODO: catch JSON parsing failure only, returning 400/'invalid json'.\n  // TODO: predicate failure returns 400/'invalid order'.\n  // TODO: success returns 200 with JSON.stringify({ total: total(order) }).\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// batch.ts\nimport { isOrder, total, type Order } from './domain/order';\n/** Validate each item at this boundary and price directly.\n * Preserve order, duplicates, empty input, and first invalid-item failure.\n * Never mutate caller input or publish a partial result.\n */\nexport function runBatch(orders: Order[]): number[] {\n  // TODO: per item, reject with Error('invalid order') or call total directly.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// index.ts\nexport { handle } from './adapters/http';\nexport { runBatch } from './batch';\nexport type { Order } from './domain/order';\n```\n\nThe type describes field shape and gives compiler-checked vocabulary; it cannot encode positivity or integrality. Brands or private constructors would violate the required structural `Order`. Runtime narrowing at each actual boundary is therefore necessary. `isOrder` accepts unknown so HTTP never asserts a parsed payload is already valid. Batch retains `Order[]` for its caller contract while guarding malformed runtime items. No `any`, assertion cast, or optional-field escape hatch is needed.\n\nThe dominant access patterns need no map, index, cache, or persistent collection. HTTP parses one object, narrows it, calculates and serializes one result. Batch traverses input once and builds one local result array. Invoice traverses two known orders through the same batch function. Results derive from inputs; nothing is synchronized.\n\n`handle` hides parsing, narrowing, HTTP errors and serialization behind one operation. `runBatch` hides validation, failure semantics, ordered traversal and arithmetic behind one operation. `total` is necessarily a small domain operation mandated by the target, not an invented abstraction. The invariant predicate pays for its small interface by removing two competing rule definitions. The facade has no runtime forwarding functions; it only exports symbols. A pricing question requires a boundary file and `domain/order.ts`; facade traversal adds no decision. Per laziness-protocol, there is no pricing service, repository, dispatcher or generic pipeline.\n\nNo mutable state is shared. Each invocation owns its result array and reads caller-owned orders synchronously without retaining them. HTTP and batch can run concurrently without locks because neither writes shared input. Per separate-before-serializing-shared-state, adding a global result cache or shared progress record would introduce an unnecessary writer conflict.\n\nBoth operations are repeatable for stable ordinary data. A crash loses local computation, with no persisted partial batch to reconcile and no external side effect to duplicate. A retry recalculates the same results; thrown validation errors publish no partial return. These are deterministic computations, not idempotent remote commands. The migration itself should resume from inspected files or restore its pristine case-owned copy, then reapply the desired final tree and rerun checks. No checkpoint database or recovery protocol is justified, per make-operations-idempotent.\n\n## Synthesis decision\n\nThis runner recommends candidate A as a base because one invariant definition and two direct callers make rule changes coherent while preserving independent transport responsibilities. The coordinator must compare actual completed candidates and fill in the final base, grafts and rejections. This document cannot claim to have inspected another runner or a judge result.\n\n## Tradeoffs accepted\n\n- We accept a small exported domain predicate in exchange for a single rule owner that both boundaries can execute.\n- We accept runtime checks at both independent boundaries in exchange for protecting each entry point; the predicate is shared and `total` trusts its input.\n- We accept a structural type that permits invalid numeric values at compile time because the target fixes `Order` to two numeric fields. Runtime boundary checks enforce the stronger rules.\n- We accept direct internal relative imports within this private package in exchange for keeping the required tree and avoiding an unrelated packaging overhaul. This sketch does not claim build-enforced file privacy.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n| Whole shape | Capability hidden and caller burden | Decision |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Shared domain predicate with independent boundaries | Callers invoke one operation; rule owner hides numeric policy, adapters hide transport/failure policy | Recommended; meets fixed tree with one invariant definition |\n| Independent HTTP and batch validators | Same narrow caller interface, but implementers must coordinate duplicated validity definitions across files | Reject; a single-file rule edit can silently change only one boundary |\n| Domain factory creates opaque validated orders, followed by trusted operations | Factory hides validity; internal callers must obtain a branded object and manage construction failures | Reject for this target; replaces its structural Order contract and makes the batch boundary contract awkward |\n| Unified pricing service accepts transport-tagged commands | One service hides dispatch and validation, but callers learn tagged HTTP/batch variants and the domain gains transport knowledge | Reject; less interface compression for this small package and wrong dependency direction |\n\n## Open questions and risks\n\nCan implementation preserve `Number.isInteger` exactly, including zero-price acceptance and rejection of noninteger or nonfinite fields, rather than quietly adding safe-integer limits? The baseline allows very large integers whose product can overflow. HTTP JSON serializes nonfinite totals as null; direct batch arithmetic can return Infinity. The named target's direct-computation requirement governs that unsupported extreme, but the supplied tests cover only ordinary values. Should any future numeric-policy change receive its own explicit contract change?\n\nWill a future package boundary need enforced exports? Today the complete private package has only the named consumers. `index.ts` omits `isOrder` and `total`, but TypeScript relative imports can still reach them. Claiming unreachable internals would be false without a build boundary. A future public package can declare exports and checks as a separate change.\n\nRed-flag screening of all eight checks:\n\n| Flag | Disposition |\n| --- | --- |\n| Shallow module | Required total is small; no extra service/wrapper. Predicate consolidates policy; boundaries hide meaningful behavior. |\n| Information leakage | Index exports domain Order only; JSON/status types stay at HTTP. Predicate has no protocol details. |\n| Temporal decomposition | Modules own domain knowledge and entry-point adaptation, rather than parse/validate/compute stage files. |\n| Pass-through method | No forwarding functions. Index reexports; boundaries perform distinct work before total. |\n| Split ownership | One numeric invariant definition in isOrder. No mutable pricing state or competing writers. |\n| Two ways to do one task | Both boundaries call total directly; remove legacy and all HTTP-via-batch routes in the same migration. |\n| Importable internals | Residual limitation explicit above. No accidental facade exports; no unverified enforcement claim. |\n| Hand-synced list | No duplicated validation tables, schema list or result cache. Ordered output derives from input. |\n\n## Next implementation step\n\nIn an authorized case-owned implementation copy, preserve the pristine baseline, introduce `domain/order.ts`, check the unit in isolation, switch HTTP/batch and consumer, delete legacy/update exports, then run installed strict compiler and Bun contracts plus inspect imports and absence of legacy references before declaring completion.\n\nVerification of this artifact is a source/read-based design review only. Static, runtime and dependency checks of a final implementation remain pending. No working bodies, input edits, commits or external actions were performed.\n"

      "text": "# Candidate B: boundary-owned validation, executable equivalence\n\nAssignment id: boundary-candidate-b-01\nInput revision: 9eaef39a5a653587635f687ba29282b150acb743\nresultMethod: architect-arena\nWorker: /root/boundary/candidate_b\nRequested model and effort: inherit-parent. Observed model and effort: unknown. Independent assigned worker; model diversity is unverified. No other candidate or rubric read.\n\nPhase list: Ground complete from supplied inspected how/why artifacts plus direct baseline reads; Sketch complete here; Agree and arena synthesis belong to coordinator; Implement outside this design-only assignment; Scrap conditional on later evidence. No task-list tool is exposed. Applied frozen architect and its host, model and assignment contracts, runner prompt, rationale and red-flag references, boundary-discipline, encode-lessons-in-structure, separate-before-serializing-shared-state, make-operations-idempotent, laziness-protocol, minimize-reader-load, exhaust-the-design-space and unslop. Direct invocation is unavailable; this runner performs the candidate workflow inline. Grounding and synthesis are supplied coordinator artifacts, not newly claimed worker exercises.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe baseline `legacy.ts` makes batch serialize orders into HTTP, then deserialize responses to recover multiplication results. The named target requires independent boundaries over a pure domain. Preserve `handle`'s literal responses, `runBatch`'s empty result and invalid-order error, and `invoiceTotals()` returning `[250,99]`. There are no external compatibility consumers. Historical motivation is unknown. Existing reuse plausibly avoided duplicated checks, but that is inference from the code rather than author intent.\n\n## Usage (caller's view)\n\nThese are the three intended call sites, written before the type sketch.\n\n```ts\n// HTTP entry, protocol types remain private to its adapter.\nimport { handle } from './index';\nconst response = handle('{\"quantity\":2,\"unitPrice\":125}');\n// { status: 200, body: '{\"total\":250}' }\n// handle('{') => { status: 400, body: 'invalid json' }\n// handle('null') => { status: 400, body: 'invalid order' }\n```\n\n```ts\n// Batch caller does not coordinate parsing, validation or pricing.\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from './index';\nconst orders: Order[] = [{ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 }];\nconst totals: number[] = runBatch(orders); // [250]\n// runBatch([{ quantity: 0, unitPrice: 125 }]) throws Error('invalid order').\n```\n\n```ts\n// consumer.ts\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from './index';\nexport function invoiceTotals(): number[] {\n  const orders: Order[] = [\n    { quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 }, { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 },\n  ];\n  return runBatch(orders); // [250, 99]\n}\n```\n\n## Shape\n\nUse the exact named production tree. Domain owns only `Order` and arithmetic. Each entry point owns its private validation and error translation. A single shared table in `contract.test.mjs` owns acceptance expectations and exercises both entry points. There is no shared production predicate. This deliberately exchanges code deduplication for independent boundary knowledge, per boundary-discipline. Predicate equivalence is enforced by executable cases, not claimed as a compile-time theorem.\n\n```ts\n// domain/order.ts\nexport type Order = { quantity: number; unitPrice: number };\n/** Pure multiplication. Caller has crossed a validated boundary. No mutation. */\nexport function total(order: Order): number {\n  // TODO return quantity * unitPrice, with no protocol or validation decisions.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// adapters/http.ts\nimport { total, type Order } from '../domain/order';\n// Private helper, not exported. Narrow unknown without assertions.\n/** Positive integer quantity; nonnegative integer price; ignore extra fields. */\nfunction isHttpOrder(value: unknown): value is Order {\n  // TODO guard null and inspect numeric fields with Number.isInteger.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n/** Private structural response representation. Keep wire types out of index. */\nexport function handle(raw: string): { status: number; body: string } {\n  // TODO parse unknown; catch syntax errors only => 400/'invalid json'.\n  // TODO reject invalid parsed value => 400/'invalid order'.\n  // TODO return 200 and JSON.stringify({ total: total(validatedOrder) }).\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// batch.ts\nimport { total, type Order } from './domain/order';\nfunction isBatchOrder(value: unknown): value is Order {\n  // TODO same integer and sign contract, independently implemented here.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n/** Local results only. Invalid input throws Error('invalid order'). */\nexport function runBatch(orders: Order[]): number[] {\n  // TODO map input in order, validate each item, call total directly.\n  // TODO never JSON.stringify, JSON.parse, handle, or translate HTTP status.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// index.ts, declarative facade, no additional wrapper functions\nexport { handle } from './adapters/http';\nexport { runBatch } from './batch';\nexport type { Order } from './domain/order';\n```\n\nThese snippets specify signatures and intent; they do not supply working implementations. `Order` preserves the exact structural shape required by the target. It prevents missing or nonnumeric fields in ordinary typed callers, but does not encode positivity or integrality. Local guards narrow unknown at each trust boundary. A branded exported Order would contradict the named target and break ordinary structural callers. Internals trust validated values; total performs no second validation.\n\n| Module | Knowledge and dependency |\n| --- | --- |\n| domain/order.ts | Order and multiplication only; imports nothing |\n| adapters/http.ts | JSON parsing, private guard and HTTP literals; imports domain directly |\n| batch.ts | Per-item guard, fail-fast batch behavior and result order; imports domain directly |\n| index.ts | Stable handle/runBatch exports and domain Order type; no RawOrder |\n| consumer.ts | Invoice inputs and public facade call |\n| contract.test.mjs | One shared acceptance table and adapter-specific outcome assertions |\n| legacy.ts | Deleted, no replacement compatibility copy |\n\nHTTP follows input string, parsed unknown, locally validated Order, total, encoded result. Batch follows each in-memory Order through its local guard directly into total, with totals in the input order. No index, cache or extra lookup is needed. Runtime understanding needs the boundary and domain files. The facade is a declarative entry point, not another processing layer, per laziness-protocol and minimize-reader-load.\n\nThe adapter interfaces each hide a complete operation behind one call. HTTP hides syntax errors, field checks and response encoding. Batch hides per-item checks, fail-fast failure and ordered totals. `total` is intentionally small because the mandated pricing rule is multiplication; adding a service or factory would not hide meaningful work. HTTP wire details exist only at its explicit transport operation and are not re-exported as named public types. Domain and batch contain no wire concepts.\n\n### Ownership and executable equivalence\n\nThe acceptance specification has one owner, the contract table. The HTTP and batch modules each own enforcement at their respective boundaries; this is duplicated enforcement, not independent mutable copies of pricing state. Arithmetic has one production owner, total. Each call owns its local response/results. Callers own input arrays, which neither boundary mutates. Concurrent calls have no shared writes, per separate-before-serializing-shared-state.\n\nExtend the existing test with one table of expected accept/reject decisions. Both adapter assertions derive from that table in the same test loop. Include positive quantity and zero price, fractional values, zero/negative quantity, negative price, absent fields, strings, null, extra keys and non-finite numeric field values. Derive HTTP raw input with JSON.stringify where representable; non-finite fields become null and must reject in either boundary. Keep malformed JSON as an HTTP-only test. Test successful totals against explicit expected amounts, not another implementation of multiplication. Keep the supplied literal assertions intact. Property-based expansion could follow if justified, but this change needs no extra dependency.\n\nA finite table can miss drift. Add a boundary-policy change gate: every acceptance-policy change must add its distinguishing input to this same table and exercise both adapters. Source dependency checks fail if batch imports HTTP, uses JSON, or if domain imports adapters. This is a proposed check integration, not a claim of existing enforcement, per encode-lessons-in-structure. Test helpers remain test-local. Do not export either guard or a second acceptance list.\n\n### Red-flag screen\n\n| Red flag | Assessment and response |\n| --- | --- |\n| Shallow module | total is necessarily small; each boundary completes a meaningful operation. No service wrappers. |\n| Information leakage | Only Order crosses into domain. HTTP response shape belongs to its explicit transport call. No exported RawOrder or wire alias. |\n| Temporal decomposition | No separate parse/validate/transform pipeline modules; each boundary owns its whole external contract. |\n| Pass-through method | Facade uses re-exports; no forwarding function. Consumer owns real invoice input data. |\n| Split ownership | Acceptance code is duplicated at separate boundaries, an explicit risk. One table defines required behavior; total alone owns arithmetic. No shared mutable state. |\n| Two ways to do one task | Remove legacy and RawOrder exports in the same migration. Each transport has one route into total. |\n| Importable internals | Guards stay unexported. Domain total is intentionally callable by package boundaries. Static dependency check limits adapter imports to facade/tests. No invented package export boundary in this private fixture. |\n| Hand-synced list | Contract cases live in one table and drive both adapters. No second case list or validator registry. |\n\n### Idempotency and crash behavior\n\nRepeated calls over unchanged inputs produce the same result or error. A crash during batch loses only local results; no partial output escapes and no external side effect requires compensation. Replaying a call is safe. Inputs with getters or caller mutation are outside the plain Order contract; no promise is made about adversarial object behavior. Migration itself is not a runtime transaction. Preserve the pristine copy, inspect actual files after interruption, and rerun every final check before calling the resumed migration complete. Do not replay external operations because none are part of this design, per make-operations-idempotent.\n\n## Synthesis decision\n\nThis candidate recommends boundary-owned production validation with shared executable contract cases. The coordinator's independent judge and synthesis are pending. No claim is made that B won, that other candidates were inspected, or that model diversity was observed.\n\n## Tradeoffs accepted\n\n- We accept two short guard implementations in exchange for a domain with only the required data shape and computation, plus boundary code that can be understood in isolation.\n- We accept finite test coverage rather than a proof of validator equivalence in exchange for avoiding a production validation module. A missed edge case is the main failure mode, and shared cases must expand whenever policy changes.\n- We accept structural Order values that can contain invalid numbers in exchange for preserving the required public shape. Runtime checks belong at the two entry points.\n- We accept scoped intermediate import breakage in exchange for deleting the old architecture without temporary compatibility layers.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n| Alternative | Interface depth and rejection |\n| --- | --- |\n| Domain smart constructor with branded Order | Hides validation once, but exposes construction coordination to every caller and violates the target structural Order shape if branding is required. |\n| Shared production predicate beside Order | Gives both boundaries a single policy function with a small interface and stronger drift prevention. It is viable and would be preferable if acceptance policy becomes complex; rejected here to keep domain limited to Order/total and boundary decisions local. |\n| Pricing service that parses transport inputs | Hides more implementation behind one method but exposes wire concepts to the service and makes batch depend on HTTP semantics again. Reject. |\n| Keep legacy with forwarding wrappers | Superficially minimizes disruption, but retains two routes and the transport coupling. Violates explicit deletion requirement. |\n\n## Open questions and risks\n\n- Will acceptance policy grow beyond the current two numeric checks, making a shared production predicate worth the additional ownership boundary?\n- Should integer overflow or unsafe integers become invalid in a later explicitly authorized contract change? This migration keeps Number.isInteger and multiplication behavior, including current JSON encoding consequences.\n- Are additional non-JSON batch values part of the intended contract? The signature remains Order[]; defensive per-item checks handle malformed runtime inputs, but arbitrary serialization quirks are not retained as a new API promise.\n\nNo answer is required to finish this design. Existing named contracts govern implementation.\n\n## Next implementation step\n\nAfter synthesis, introduce domain/order.ts in the case-owned working copy, check total in isolation, then switch both boundaries and consumer, delete legacy and update exports, and require the installed strict compiler, Bun runtime contracts and explicit dependency/no-legacy inspection before reporting implementation complete.\n\nDesign verification here consists of reading all six named baseline files, grounding/how/why outputs and frozen references, and reconciling the three call sites to the proposed signatures. Baseline pass claims come from the grounding artifact. No new runtime or static execution and no working-source edit occurred in this candidate. Only this assigned result file was written.\n"

      "path": "fixtures/cases/boundary-design/outputs/synthesis.md",

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Synthesized design: shared invariant and independent boundary enforcement\n\nCoordinator /root/boundary. Input revision9eaef39a5a653587635f687ba29282b150acb743. Frozen package0.1.3 operative51f209daf83cd97d64f89881d66533bb59dc57db. Candidate A and B completed and inspected; fresh judge completed and inspected before synthesis. Models requestedinherit,observedunknown. This is the selected scaffold contract; notimplemented signatures below document the design. Actual implemented bodies live separately in outputs/working.\n\n## Problem\n\n`legacy.ts` owns quantity/price rules, multiplication, HTTP response construction, and a batch route through JSON and HTTP. `target.md` requires direct domain calls from independent HTTP and batch boundaries, fixed filenames, stable facade calls and invoice results, and deletion of the old implementation without compatibility remnants. HTTP must keep its exact three outcomes. The shared arithmetic is multiplication. Historical reasons for HTTP reuse remain unknown; the why artifact offers reuse only as inference. The coordinator observed the pristine baseline compiler and Bun checks before design, then implemented and verified the selected sketch. The signatures below retain the design-stage placeholders; working source contains complete bodies.\n\n## Usage (caller's view)\n\nThe README shows existing consumers importing the same facade functions and the new domain type. Callers need no parser, policy object, dependency injection, validator sequencing, or HTTP response knowledge to use batch pricing.\n\n```ts\n// HTTP call site, such as a request bridge.\nimport { handle } from './index';\nconst response = handle('{\"quantity\":2,\"unitPrice\":125}');\n// { status: 200, body: '{\"total\":250}' }\n// handle('{') returns { status: 400, body: 'invalid json' }.\n// handle('null') returns { status: 400, body: 'invalid order' }.\n```\n\n```ts\n// Batch call site.\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from './index';\nconst orders: Order[] = [{ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 }];\nconst totals = runBatch(orders); // [250]\n// runBatch([]) returns []. Invalid items throw Error('invalid order').\n```\n\n```ts\n// consumer.ts, migrated to domain vocabulary.\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from \"./index\";\nexport function invoiceTotals(): number[] {\n  const orders: Order[] = [\n    { quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 },\n    { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 },\n  ];\n  return runBatch(orders); // [250,99]\n}\n```\n\n## Shape\n\nKeep the named final tree exactly. One transport-independent predicate in `domain/order.ts` owns the quantity/price invariant; HTTP and batch each execute that predicate at their own boundary. The domain calculator never repeats it. Transport adaptation belongs to the boundary; the rule itself belongs to the domain. This resolves duplicate policy without moving JSON or HTTP into the domain, per boundary-discipline and minimize-reader-load.\n\n```text\ndomain/order.ts    Order, isOrder, total; imports nothing\nadapters/http.ts  handle; imports isOrder and total from ../domain/order\nbatch.ts          runBatch; imports Order, isOrder, total from ./domain/order\nindex.ts          exports handle, runBatch, and type Order only\nconsumer.ts       invoiceTotals; imports runBatch and type Order from ./index\nlegacy.ts         deleted; no remaining aliases or copies\n```\n\nData structures and signatures, with bodies intentionally absent:\n\n```ts\n// domain/order.ts\nexport type Order = { quantity: number; unitPrice: number };\n/** Sole owner of integer quantity > 0 and integer unitPrice >= 0.\n * Accept unknown, narrow only after observing both fields.\n * No JSON parsing, status codes, transport errors, or mutation.\n */\nexport function isOrder(value: unknown): value is Order {\n  // TODO: express the baseline quantity/price predicate once.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n/** Pure arithmetic. Trust its typed input; do not validate again. */\nexport function total(order: Order): number {\n  // TODO: multiply quantity by unitPrice.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// adapters/http.ts\nimport { isOrder, total } from '../domain/order';\n/** Parse into unknown; distinguish parse failure from invalid order.\n * Call total only after isOrder succeeds. Preserve literal bodies.\n */\nexport function handle(raw: string): { status: number; body: string } {\n  // TODO: catch JSON parsing failure only, returning 400/'invalid json'.\n  // TODO: predicate failure returns 400/'invalid order'.\n  // TODO: success returns 200 with JSON.stringify({ total: total(order) }).\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// batch.ts\nimport { isOrder, total, type Order } from './domain/order';\n/** Validate each item at this boundary and price directly.\n * Preserve order, duplicates, empty input, and first invalid-item failure.\n * Never mutate caller input or publish a partial result.\n */\nexport function runBatch(orders: Order[]): number[] {\n  // TODO: per item, reject with Error('invalid order') or call total directly.\n  throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n\n// index.ts\nexport { handle } from './adapters/http';\nexport { runBatch } from './batch';\nexport type { Order } from './domain/order';\n```\n\nThe type describes field shape and gives compiler-checked vocabulary; it cannot encode positivity or integrality. Brands or private constructors would violate the required structural `Order`. Runtime narrowing at each actual boundary is therefore necessary. `isOrder` accepts unknown so HTTP never asserts a parsed payload is already valid. Batch retains `Order[]` for its caller contract while guarding malformed runtime items. No `any`, assertion cast, or optional-field escape hatch is needed.\n\nThe dominant access patterns need no map, index, cache, or persistent collection. HTTP parses one object, narrows it, calculates and serializes one result. Batch traverses input once and builds one local result array. Invoice traverses two known orders through the same batch function. Results derive from inputs; nothing is synchronized.\n\n`handle` hides parsing, narrowing, HTTP errors and serialization behind one operation. `runBatch` hides validation, failure semantics, ordered traversal and arithmetic behind one operation. `total` is necessarily a small domain operation mandated by the target, not an invented abstraction. The invariant predicate pays for its small interface by removing two competing rule definitions. The facade has no runtime forwarding functions; it only exports symbols. A pricing question requires a boundary file and `domain/order.ts`; facade traversal adds no decision. Per laziness-protocol, there is no pricing service, repository, dispatcher or generic pipeline.\n\nNo mutable state is shared. Each invocation owns its result array and reads caller-owned orders synchronously without retaining them. HTTP and batch can run concurrently without locks because neither writes shared input. Per separate-before-serializing-shared-state, adding a global result cache or shared progress record would introduce an unnecessary writer conflict.\n\nBoth operations are repeatable for stable ordinary data. A crash loses local computation, with no persisted partial batch to reconcile and no external side effect to duplicate. A retry recalculates the same results; thrown validation errors publish no partial return. These are deterministic computations, not idempotent remote commands. The migration itself should resume from inspected files or restore its pristine case-owned copy, then reapply the desired final tree and rerun checks. No checkpoint database or recovery protocol is justified, per make-operations-idempotent.\n\n## Synthesis decision\nBase A. Fresh judge /root/boundary/judge recommends A23/25 over B22/25; coordinator independently favors A24/25 over B21/25. Both prefer one production invariant definition and direct total calls. Judge scores target placement lower for A; resolve explicitly: isOrder defines transport-independent numeric policy, while HTTP and batch execute validation at their own external entry boundary. total remains pure and unchecked. No parsing,HTTP statuses or transport types enter domain/order.ts.\n\nGraft from B its complete invoice caller example and one shared acceptance-case matrix with explicit expected totals into supplemental tests. Preserve original contract.test.mjs byte-for-byte. Add observed dependency checks. Reject B duplicated production guards because finite cases cannot prevent untested policy drift; reject its procedural change gate as a substitute for one definition. Reject brands/factories altering structural Order, transport-tagged services and legacy compatibility routes. No dropouts. All8 red flags compared in candidate tables and judge. Importable internals remains an explicit private-package limitation; selected dependency checks do not imply full import privacy. Fixed target limits structural diversity to invariant placement/enforcement.\n\n## Tradeoffs accepted\n\n- We accept a small exported domain predicate in exchange for a single rule owner that both boundaries can execute.\n- We accept runtime checks at both independent boundaries in exchange for protecting each entry point; the predicate is shared and `total` trusts its input.\n- We accept a structural type that permits invalid numeric values at compile time because the target fixes `Order` to two numeric fields. Runtime boundary checks enforce the stronger rules.\n- We accept direct internal relative imports within this private package in exchange for keeping the required tree and avoiding an unrelated packaging overhaul. This sketch does not claim build-enforced file privacy.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\n| Whole shape | Capability hidden and caller burden | Decision |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Shared domain predicate with independent boundaries | Callers invoke one operation; rule owner hides numeric policy, adapters hide transport/failure policy | Recommended; meets fixed tree with one invariant definition |\n| Independent HTTP and batch validators | Same narrow caller interface, but implementers must coordinate duplicated validity definitions across files | Reject; a single-file rule edit can silently change only one boundary |\n| Domain factory creates opaque validated orders, followed by trusted operations | Factory hides validity; internal callers must obtain a branded object and manage construction failures | Reject for this target; replaces its structural Order contract and makes the batch boundary contract awkward |\n| Unified pricing service accepts transport-tagged commands | One service hides dispatch and validation, but callers learn tagged HTTP/batch variants and the domain gains transport knowledge | Reject; less interface compression for this small package and wrong dependency direction |\n\n## Open questions and risks\n\nCan implementation preserve `Number.isInteger` exactly, including zero-price acceptance and rejection of noninteger or nonfinite fields, rather than quietly adding safe-integer limits? The baseline allows very large integers whose product can overflow. HTTP JSON serializes nonfinite totals as null; direct batch arithmetic can return Infinity. The named target's direct-computation requirement governs that unsupported extreme, but the supplied tests cover only ordinary values. Should any future numeric-policy change receive its own explicit contract change?\n\nWill a future package boundary need enforced exports? Today the complete private package has only the named consumers. `index.ts` omits `isOrder` and `total`, but TypeScript relative imports can still reach them. Claiming unreachable internals would be false without a build boundary. A future public package can declare exports and checks as a separate change.\n\nRed-flag screening of all eight checks:\n\n| Flag | Disposition |\n| --- | --- |\n| Shallow module | Required total is small; no extra service/wrapper. Predicate consolidates policy; boundaries hide meaningful behavior. |\n| Information leakage | Index exports domain Order only; JSON/status types stay at HTTP. Predicate has no protocol details. |\n| Temporal decomposition | Modules own domain knowledge and entry-point adaptation, rather than parse/validate/compute stage files. |\n| Pass-through method | No forwarding functions. Index reexports; boundaries perform distinct work before total. |\n| Split ownership | One numeric invariant definition in isOrder. No mutable pricing state or competing writers. |\n| Two ways to do one task | Both boundaries call total directly; remove legacy and all HTTP-via-batch routes in the same migration. |\n| Importable internals | Residual limitation explicit above. No accidental facade exports; no unverified enforcement claim. |\n| Hand-synced list | No duplicated validation tables, schema list or result cache. Ordered output derives from input. |\n\n## Next implementation step\n\nIn an authorized case-owned implementation copy, preserve the pristine baseline, introduce `domain/order.ts`, check the unit in isolation, switch HTTP/batch and consumer, delete legacy/update exports, then run installed strict compiler and Bun contracts plus inspect imports and absence of legacy references before declaring completion.\n\nImplementation authorization was supplied with no checkpoint or commit requested. The coordinator has implemented this sketch in outputs/working. Pure domain isolated checks, strict installed compiler and Bun4tests35assertions returned exit0. Dependency/source and pristine checks are recorded separately in verification.json. No repeated workaround or unanticipated parameter arose; no scrap cycle was triggered. If two independent same-shape deviations arise later, re-ground and re-run arena instead of patching the wrong shape.\n"

      "text": "import { isOrder, total } from \"../domain/order\";\n\nexport function handle(raw: string): { status: number; body: string } {\n  let order: unknown;\n  try {\n    order = JSON.parse(raw);\n  } catch {\n    return { status: 400, body: \"invalid json\" };\n  }\n  if (!isOrder(order)) return { status: 400, body: \"invalid order\" };\n  return { status: 200, body: JSON.stringify({ total: total(order) }) };\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/boundary-design/outputs/working/batch.ts",
      "bytes": 239,
      "sha256": "7993ddb103041c22e25fe7b196a49463522bd9a3aaae670801b9ec81caba1837",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { isOrder, total, type Order } from \"./domain/order\";\n\nexport function runBatch(orders: Order[]): number[] {\n  return orders.map(order => {\n    if (!isOrder(order)) throw new Error(\"invalid order\");\n    return total(order);\n  });\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/boundary-design/outputs/working/index.ts",
      "bytes": 123,
      "sha256": "4632e3a58bc3f06a23dfd8104e3c4c239ea3db22ba10ceecbcf29c8b8c9538e2",

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export { handle } from \"./adapters/http\";\nexport { runBatch } from \"./batch\";\nexport type { Order } from \"./domain/order\";\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/boundary-design/outputs/working/consumer.ts",
      "bytes": 228,
      "sha256": "ff5d6e8aa45ea0805c7d0d513eddc38d222436da343e132386b0f5058e673d1f",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { runBatch, type Order } from \"./index\";\n\nexport function invoiceTotals(): number[] {\n  const orders: Order[] = [\n    { quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 },\n    { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 },\n  ];\n  return runBatch(orders);\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/boundary-design/outputs/working/contract.test.mjs",
      "bytes": 761,
      "sha256": "b4c462080c43283cdc7b8ba9d10a9abccd9a7847c9c7950ea1cb06d56bfacfdc",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { test, expect } from 'bun:test';\nimport { handle, runBatch } from './index.ts';\nimport { invoiceTotals } from './consumer.ts';\ntest('stable HTTP success and error contract', () => {\n  expect(handle('{\"quantity\":2,\"unitPrice\":125}')).toEqual({ status: 200, body: '{\"total\":250}' });\n  expect(handle('{')).toEqual({ status: 400, body: 'invalid json' });\n  for (const raw of ['null', '{}', '{\"quantity\":0,\"unitPrice\":125}', '{\"quantity\":1,\"unitPrice\":-1}']) expect(handle(raw)).toEqual({ status: 400, body: 'invalid order' });\n});\ntest('private batch and consumer contracts', () => {\n  expect(runBatch([])).toEqual([]);\n  expect(invoiceTotals()).toEqual([250, 99]);\n  expect(() => runBatch([{ quantity: 0, unitPrice: 125 }])).toThrow('invalid order');\n});\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "fixtures/cases/boundary-design/outputs/working/boundary-policy.test.mjs",
      "bytes": 1603,
      "sha256": "68d8085f6767a131fa8b37be0022d82a8b41b9df4bab815d80d30c4a616740b3",
      "owner": "local-013-initial-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { test, expect } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { handle, runBatch } from \"./index.ts\";\n\nconst cases = [\n  { order: { quantity: 3, unitPrice: 0 }, expected: 0 },\n  { order: { quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125, extra: true }, expected: 250 },\n  { order: { quantity: 1.5, unitPrice: 2 } },\n  { order: { quantity: -1, unitPrice: 2 } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 0.5 } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1, unitPrice: \"2\" } },\n  { order: { quantity: Infinity, unitPrice: 2 } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1, unitPrice: NaN } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1 } },\n  { order: null },\n  { order: [] },\n];\n\ntest(\"both entry boundaries enforce the shared acceptance contract\", () => {\n  for (const { order, expected } of cases) {\n    const response = handle(JSON.stringify(order));\n    if (expected !== undefined) {\n      expect(response).toEqual({ status: 200, body: JSON.stringify({ total: expected }) });\n      expect(runBatch([order])).toEqual([expected]);\n    } else {\n      expect(response).toEqual({ status: 400, body: \"invalid order\" });\n      expect(() => runBatch([order])).toThrow(\"invalid order\");\n    }\n  }\n});\n\ntest(\"batch preserves ordering and inputs and rejects an invalid later item\", () => {\n  const first = Object.freeze({ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 });\n  const second = Object.freeze({ quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 });\n  const input = [first, second, first];\n  expect(runBatch(input)).toEqual([250, 99, 250]);\n  expect(runBatch(input)).toEqual([250, 99, 250]);\n  expect(input).toEqual([first, second, first]);\n  expect(() => runBatch([first, { quantity: 0, unitPrice: 5 }])).toThrow(\"invalid order\");\n});\n"
    },

The signatures below retain the design-stage placeholders; working source contains complete bodies.\\n\\n## Usage (caller's view)\\n\\nThe README shows existing consumers importing the same facade functions and the new domain type.       "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import {runBatch,type Order} from './working/index';\nimport {invoiceTotals} from './working/consumer';\nimport {total} from './working/domain/order';\ntype Equal<A,B>=(<T>()=>T extends A?1:2) extends (<T>()=>T extends B?1:2)?true:false;\nconst batchExact: Equal<ReturnType<typeof runBatch>,Array<number|null>> = true;\nconst callerExact: Equal<ReturnType<typeof invoiceTotals>,Array<number|null>> = true;\nconst domainExact: Equal<ReturnType<typeof total>,number> = true;\nconst order:Order={quantity:1e308,unitPrice:2};\nconst results:Array<number|null>=runBatch([order]);\nconst invoices:Array<number|null>=invoiceTotals();\nconst arithmetic:number=total(order);\n// @ts-expect-error The batch contract requires callers to account for null.\nconst inaccurateBatch:number[]=runBatch([order]);\n// @ts-expect-error The application caller carries the same nullable result.\nconst inaccurateCaller:number[]=invoiceTotals();\nvoid [batchExact,callerExact,domainExact,results,invoices,arithmetic,inaccurateBatch,inaccurateCaller];\n"
    },

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import {test,expect} from 'bun:test';\nimport {handle,runBatch} from './working/index.ts';\nimport {invoiceTotals} from './working/consumer.ts';\nimport {total} from './working/domain/order.ts';\nimport * as legacy from '../reference/legacy.ts';\n\nconst valid=[{quantity:2,unitPrice:125},{quantity:3,unitPrice:0},{quantity:1,unitPrice:99},{quantity:1e308,unitPrice:2},{quantity:2,unitPrice:1e308},{quantity:1e308,unitPrice:0}];\nfor(const order of valid)test('reference parity '+JSON.stringify(order),()=>{\n  expect(runBatch([order])).toEqual(legacy.runBatch([order]));\n  expect(handle(JSON.stringify(order))).toEqual(legacy.handle(JSON.stringify(order)));\n});\nconst invalid=[null,[],{}, {quantity:0,unitPrice:2},{quantity:-1,unitPrice:2},{quantity:1.5,unitPrice:2},{quantity:1,unitPrice:-1},{quantity:1,unitPrice:0.5},{quantity:1,unitPrice:'2'},{quantity:Infinity,unitPrice:2},{quantity:-Infinity,unitPrice:2},{quantity:NaN,unitPrice:2},{quantity:1,unitPrice:Infinity},{quantity:1,unitPrice:NaN}];\nfor(const [i,order] of invalid.entries())test('invalid and nonfinite parity '+i,()=>{\n  expect(()=>runBatch([order])).toThrow('invalid order');\n  expect(()=>legacy.runBatch([order])).toThrow('invalid order');\n  expect(handle(JSON.stringify(order))).toEqual(legacy.handle(JSON.stringify(order)));\n});\nfor(const raw of ['{','null','{}','{\"quantity\":1e309,\"unitPrice\":2}','{\"quantity\":1,\"unitPrice\":1e309}','{\"quantity\":NaN,\"unitPrice\":2}'])test('HTTP raw compatibility '+raw,()=>expect(handle(raw)).toEqual(legacy.handle(raw)));\ntest('domain arithmetic remains numeric and permits overflow',()=>{\n expect(total({quantity:1e308,unitPrice:2})).toBe(Infinity);\n expect(total({quantity:2,unitPrice:125})).toBe(250);\n});\ntest('all callers, ordering, repeated calls, empty input, immutable orders',()=>{\n const orders=valid.map(order=>Object.freeze({...order}));\n const before=structuredClone(orders);\n expect(runBatch(orders)).toEqual(legacy.runBatch(orders));\n expect(runBatch(orders)).toEqual(legacy.runBatch(orders));\n expect(orders).toEqual(before);\n expect(runBatch([])).toEqual([]);\n expect(invoiceTotals()).toEqual([250,99]);\n expect(()=>runBatch([valid[0],invalid[0]])).toThrow('invalid order');\n});\n"
