      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Candidate B: invocation-local batch collection\n\n## Problem\n\nThe broken input exposes arithmetic Infinity from batch while the pristine reference returns null through JSON serialization. Both finite integer operand positions overflow in the observed literal RED result. The reference's number[] annotation is inaccurate. input/target.md requires nullable batch and consumer results, unchanged domain arithmetic, unchanged HTTP behavior, and a direct runBatch call to total. outputs/how.md traces these boundaries; outputs/why.md grounds batch ownership in the current target and leaves the historical intention behind legacy null unknown.\n\n## Usage (caller's view)\n\nConsumers import the same entry points and retain ordinary results. Overflow becomes an explicit nullable result. HTTP keeps its existing string protocol.\n\n```ts\nimport { runBatch, handle, type Order } from \"./index\";\n\n// A regular application batch.\nconst orders: Order[] = [{ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 }];\nconst totals = runBatch(orders); // [250], Array<number | null>\n\n// A report must handle a finite-input arithmetic overflow.\nconst report = runBatch([{ quantity: 1e308, unitPrice: 2 }]);\nfor (const value of report) {\n  const display = value === null ? \"overflow\" : String(value);\n}\n\n// Existing HTTP consumers retain status and JSON response behavior.\nconst response = handle('{\"quantity\":2,\"unitPrice\":125}');\n// { status: 200, body: '{\"total\":250}' }\n```\n\nThe repository's invoiceTotals caller keeps its existing import and order creation, and declares Array<number | null>. Its current ordinary result remains [250, 99]. Invalid orders still throw Error(\"invalid order\") from runBatch. Callers receive a complete array or an exception, never a partially returned batch.\n\n## Shape\n\nUse a private BatchTotals collection in batch.ts. Its invocation-local array owns accumulated normalized totals. runBatch owns input traversal and acceptance, calls total directly, then hands the numeric result to the collection. The collection owns finite-result normalization and ordered result exposure. Domain total remains a pure number-returning multiplication.\n\n```ts\n// batch.ts, internal declaration, not exported.\nclass BatchTotals {\n  #values: Array<number | null> = [];\n\n  /** Append exactly one boundary-normalized value in input order. */\n  append(value: number): void {\n    // TODO: push value when Number.isFinite(value), otherwise null.\n    throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n  }\n\n  /** Return a snapshot; callers cannot reach the mutable collection. */\n  snapshot(): Array<number | null> {\n    // TODO: return a shallow copy of #values.\n    throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n  }\n}\n\n// batch.ts, the existing public batch function.\nexport function runBatch(orders: Order[]): Array<number | null> {\n  // TODO: create a fresh BatchTotals for this invocation.\n  // TODO: for each order, reject !isOrder with Error(\"invalid order\").\n  // TODO: call total(order) here directly, then append its numeric result.\n  // TODO: return the collection snapshot after every order succeeds.\n  throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n}\n\n// consumer.ts, existing caller.\nexport function invoiceTotals(): Array<number | null> {\n  // TODO: keep existing orders and return runBatch(orders).\n  throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n}\n\n// Existing domain contract and implementation remain unchanged.\nexport function total(order: Order): number; // existing body remains multiplication\n```\n\n| Module | Knowledge and ownership |\n| --- | --- |\n| domain/order.ts | Order shape, isOrder acceptance, pure total arithmetic. No changes. |\n| adapters/http.ts | JSON parse/stringify and HTTP status/body. No changes. |\n| batch.ts | Acceptance at the batch boundary, direct total call, private BatchTotals normalization and invocation-local accumulated array. |\n| consumer.ts | Accurate nullable result annotation for invoiceTotals. |\n| index.ts | Existing public exports. No BatchTotals export. |\n\nThe public interface hides traversal, rejection, numeric normalization and ordered accumulation behind one runBatch call. It adds no public options or lifecycle steps. Nullable output is the only new caller obligation because the previous annotation concealed real legacy behavior. The private collection has a narrower internal interface than an exported generic accumulator, but it hides little additional complexity for the present requirements. Its main benefit is a single inaccessible writer for batch results, per separate-before-serializing-shared-state and encode-lessons-in-structure. This is a credible encapsulated shape, with a real small-module cost.\n\nAcceptance stays at each entry boundary using the shared isOrder predicate, per boundary-discipline. Do not add finite-output policy to total or normalize inside HTTP. Public result types encode nullability; TypeScript cannot encode every finite JavaScript number without extra branding, so Number.isFinite at the batch result boundary remains necessary. Batch never stringifies or parses. The whole flow stays inside batch.ts and domain/order.ts, per minimize-reader-load.\n\nEach invocation creates its own collection. Concurrent callers cannot share a writer. Running a batch twice produces the same ordered results without mutating inputs. If a later order fails, the invocation's partial collection becomes unreachable and no partial result escapes. A crash has no persistent state to recover.\n\n## Synthesis decision\n\nThis is candidate B's proposed base, not the orchestrator's synthesis. No other candidate output has been read, so no graft, rejection of another runner, or final winner is asserted. Preserve this candidate's private collection ownership as its distinct structural choice. The orchestrator must compare it with the function-local alternative, inspect both packages, and obtain a fresh judge before choosing a base. Candidate B offers the explicit single-writer collection for consideration; the extra private protocol must justify its cost.\n\n## Tradeoffs accepted\n\n- We accept a private class and two internal methods in exchange for one inaccessible owner of accumulated results and their normalization.\n- We accept a shallow array copy on successful completion in exchange for a detached result snapshot with no connection to the private writer.\n- We accept nullable caller handling in exchange for an accurate public contract that preserves pristine reference behavior.\n- We accept that the private class is a small abstraction for today's arithmetic policy in exchange for keeping that boundary policy and its mutable array together.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\nA function-local map in runBatch validates an order, calls total directly, and converts a nonfinite result to null in the callback. It has the same one-function public interface and hides the same externally relevant policy with fewer internal steps. It exposes no extra complexity to callers. It is structurally distinct because array construction belongs to map and numeric normalization belongs to the runBatch callback, with no separate collection owner. It loses within this seat's proposed shape because the finite policy and array ownership cannot be encapsulated together behind a private collection, but its lower internal cost is a strong reason for the final judge to choose it instead.\n\nAn exported BatchService with create, addOrder and finish methods would force callers to coordinate a lifecycle and expose partial mutable processing. It hides arithmetic and validation but makes the caller learn sequencing and recovery rules. Reject that shape because interface depth is worse than either single-call candidate.\n\nA domain-level nullable total would hide normalization from the batch caller but move boundary-specific compatibility into pure arithmetic, contradict total(order):number, and change both domain and HTTP ownership. Reject that shape under the supplied constraints.\n\n## Open questions and risks\n\n- Does the single-writer collection provide enough policy ownership benefit to justify two internal calls and a snapshot copy for this small subsystem, or should synthesis choose the function-local alternative?\n- Should a future reporting API distinguish overflow from other possible nonfinite arithmetic results? The current contract answers only that all nonfinite totals become null, so this repair should not add new result variants.\n- If a later requirement introduces cross-call accumulation, can it preserve invocation-local ownership rather than turning BatchTotals into shared state? That change would require fresh grounding and exploration.\n\nNone of these questions blocks the authorized repair under the current contract.\n\n## Next implementation step\n\nAfter synthesis, edit only the owned working copy's batch.ts and consumer.ts to match the chosen signatures and boundary policy, then run literal overflow regressions and the actual strict compiler before compatibility checks.\n\n## Eight design red flags\n\n| Flag | Finding and design response |\n| --- | --- |\n| Shallow module | BatchTotals has only finite normalization and ordered accumulation to hide. Keep it private with append and snapshot only; recognize its internal overhead when comparing depth with the function-local design. |\n| Information leakage | HTTP JSON/status types remain within the HTTP adapter. Batch receives Order and returns nullable numeric values. The finite policy lives only in BatchTotals within batch.ts. |\n| Temporal decomposition | No exported validate/compute/serialize pipeline appears. Batch owns boundary acceptance and output compatibility in one file; the class groups result policy with its accumulated state. |\n| Pass-through method | runBatch validates and computes; append normalizes and writes; snapshot detaches the result. No method forwards unchanged arguments merely to add a layer. |\n| Split ownership | Only BatchTotals writes its private values. Every runBatch invocation creates a different object; consumers receive snapshots. |\n| Two ways to do one task | runBatch remains the only public batch entry. Do not restore legacy or introduce a second batch helper. HTTP remains a distinct protocol entry, never a batch route. |\n| Importable internals | BatchTotals is unexported and defined inside batch.ts, so callers cannot import it through either the module or index. No internal file becomes an alternative entry. |\n| Hand-synced list | No parallel lists are introduced. isOrder remains the single acceptance predicate; tests enumerate behavior rather than maintaining a second production policy table. |\n\n## Implementation guardrails\n\nKeep this package design-only. No implementation or tests ran in this candidate. Root's observed literal RED is prior grounding, not this worker's execution evidence. Final integration must run both overflow operand positions, normal totals, invalid and nonfinite HTTP/batch behavior, consumer compatibility, every caller, and real strict TypeScript. The final tree must omit legacy. A new transport dependency, broadened domain type, shared collection, casts to hide nullable callers, or repeated special-case branches signals a deviation. Two independent deviations of the same shape require the orchestrator to scrap the sketch, reground with how and why, and repeat exploration. No checkpoint was requested; implementation is authorized at the root, while this runner remains design-only.\n\n## Receipt\n\n```json\n{\n  \"assignmentId\": \"candidate-b\",\n  \"inputRevision\": \"54c6a8f432d247f165933d990ef8654874c6a39a\",\n  \"outputOwner\": \"/root/candidate_b\",\n  \"outputPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/candidate-b/design.md\",\n  \"method\": {\n    \"sampleCount\": 1,\n    \"sampleDefinition\": \"one whole subsystem design with caller-first types and eight-flag screen\",\n    \"order\": \"usage then shape then rationale then screen\"\n  },\n  \"requestedModel\": \"inherit-parent\",\n  \"actualModel\": \"unknown\",\n  \"retryCount\": 0,\n  \"scope\": \"design-only\",\n  \"status\": \"completed\"\n}\n```\n\nRead all four exact frozen architect source files under the supplied stable package. Also read the frozen host-capabilities, model-roles, orchestration-contract and unslop instructions. Read input/target.md, every supplied input application/test/config file, reference/legacy.ts, literal-overflow.test.mjs and the supplied how/why/RED artifacts. This independent worker has not read other candidate output or the parent rubric. The model is unobserved, so this package establishes no model diversity. Direct skill invocation was not exposed; this worker executed the candidate runner discipline from the literal files. Root owns how/why execution, arena orchestration and fresh judging; this candidate does not claim those operations itself.\n"

      "text": "# Problem\nThe broken batch returns Infinity for accepted finite integer operands whose product overflows; the pristine reference returns null through JSON. Its number[] type was inaccurate. Grounding in how.md and why.md establishes current ownership constraints from input/target.md:3, not historical intent. Preserve pure total:number, shared acceptance and HTTP behavior; repair only batch compatibility and caller types.\n\n# Usage (caller's view)\n```ts\nimport {runBatch,type Order} from './index';\nconst totals:Array<number|null>=runBatch([{quantity:2,unitPrice:125}]); // [250]\nconst overflow:Array<number|null>=runBatch([{quantity:1e308,unitPrice:2}]); // [null]\n// runBatch([{quantity:0,unitPrice:2}]) throws Error('invalid order').\n```\n```ts\nimport {runBatch,type Order} from './index';\nexport function invoiceTotals():Array<number|null> {\n const orders:Order[]=[{quantity:2,unitPrice:125},{quantity:1,unitPrice:99}];\n return runBatch(orders);\n}\n```\n```ts\nimport {handle} from './index';\nconst response=handle('{\"quantity\":1e308,\"unitPrice\":2}');\n// {status:200,body:'{\"total\":null}'}\n```\n\n# Shape\nData is an invocation-local Array<number|null>, constructed by map. There is no shared state or new public export. Batch validates at entry with existing isOrder, directly calls total once per accepted order, then applies Number.isFinite to the numeric result. Domain owns Order/acceptance/arithmetic; HTTP owns parsing/status/JSON; consumer owns accurate caller annotation; index exports stay unchanged. Batch hides traversal, errors, ordering and finite policy behind one call, per boundary-discipline and minimize-reader-load. Null remains exposed because it is real behavior, per type-system-discipline. Callers receive either a complete ordered array or an exception. Partial map state is unreachable after an error; repeated calls share no state, per separate-before-serializing-shared-state.\n\n```ts\n// batch.ts\nexport function runBatch(orders:Order[]):Array<number|null> {\n // TODO map each order; reject invalid order; compute total(order) directly.\n // TODO return computed total if finite, otherwise null.\n throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n// consumer.ts\nexport function invoiceTotals():Array<number|null> {\n throw new Error('not implemented');\n}\n// domain/order.ts and HTTP adapter remain byte-identical.\nexport function total(order:Order):number; // existing pure multiplication\n```\n\n# Synthesis decision\nA is the base. Parent compared every criterion and both full artifacts; fresh judge /root/judge_1 independently recommends A. Both meet behavioral/type requirements, caller-first rationale and verification feasibility. A hides the same public complexity with less internal coordination. B is a genuinely different object-owned accumulator. Graft B's complete-array-or-error explanation and public-depth versus internal-protocol distinction into rationale only. Reject its private class and snapshot copy because map already provides invocation isolation. No candidate dropout or assignment retry occurred. Actual models are unknown; independence does not establish diversity. The detailed criterion and eight-flag comparisons are in judge/review.md.\n\n# Tradeoffs accepted\n- We accept nullable caller results in exchange for truthful legacy overflow compatibility.\n- We accept one runtime finite-result check in exchange for unchanged pure arithmetic and accepted large finite operands.\n- We accept fresh map allocation in exchange for isolated calls and unchanged inputs.\n\n# Alternatives considered\nB's private BatchTotals hides result normalization and accumulation behind append/snapshot, but runBatch must coordinate more internal steps for the same single-call public capability. It loses on interface depth inside the module. An exported create/add/finish service would expose partial processing and sequencing to callers. Nullable domain arithmetic violates the numeric domain contract; HTTP/JSON round trips restore the coupling the target forbids.\n\n# Open questions and risks\nWill literal tests and real strict compilation confirm both overflow positions and nullable callers? Will reference comparisons confirm ordinary, invalid and nonfinite HTTP/batch cases? Could a future incremental or shared-state requirement justify regrounding and a new collection design? These questions call for verification or future design work; they do not require a checkpoint for this authorized repair. No universal import boundary is claimed beyond local unimportable finite policy and intentional domain exports.\n\n# Next implementation step\nChange only outputs/working/batch.ts and consumer.ts, then run literal GREEN, compatibility, real strict compiler, caller/import/legacy inspection and immutable before/after checks.\n\n# Eight-flag decision\n| Flag | Chosen response |\n| --- | --- |\n| Shallow module | Existing batch operation hides full policy, no extra helper. |\n| Information leakage | No wire types or JSON in batch. |\n| Temporal decomposition | Whole batch operation stays in one boundary. |\n| Pass-through | Batch validates and normalizes beyond arithmetic. |\n| Split ownership | One invocation-local result, no shared writer. |\n| Two ways | One batch route, no legacy or HTTP fallback. |\n| Importable internals | Local normalization cannot be imported; domain exports intentional. |\n| Hand-synced list | No production duplicate list; derive caller inventory from actual source. |\n\n# Agreement and deviation handling\nImplementation is authorized; no opt-in checkpoint was requested.       "text": "# Repair compatibility without restoring transport coupling\nPreserve the complete architecture method in the supplied case contract. This is an existing subsystem, requiring how/why grounding, two independent structurally distinct design candidates, fresh judging, all eight red flags, caller-first usage, ownership and full rationale before implementation.\nActual legacy batch returns null for finite integer multiplication overflowing to Infinity. Native workflow must first add and execute a literal overflow regression showing red against this committed broken input, then repair and run green tests plus actual strict compiler.     {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/boundary-policy.test.mjs",
      "bytes": 1603,
      "sha256": "68d8085f6767a131fa8b37be0022d82a8b41b9df4bab815d80d30c4a616740b3",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { test, expect } from \"bun:test\";\nimport { handle, runBatch } from \"./index.ts\";\n\nconst cases = [\n  { order: { quantity: 3, unitPrice: 0 }, expected: 0 },\n  { order: { quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125, extra: true }, expected: 250 },\n  { order: { quantity: 1.5, unitPrice: 2 } },\n  { order: { quantity: -1, unitPrice: 2 } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 0.5 } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1, unitPrice: \"2\" } },\n  { order: { quantity: Infinity, unitPrice: 2 } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1, unitPrice: NaN } },\n  { order: { quantity: 1 } },\n  { order: null },\n  { order: [] },\n];\n\ntest(\"both entry boundaries enforce the shared acceptance contract\", () => {\n  for (const { order, expected } of cases) {\n    const response = handle(JSON.stringify(order));\n    if (expected !== undefined) {\n      expect(response).toEqual({ status: 200, body: JSON.stringify({ total: expected }) });\n      expect(runBatch([order])).toEqual([expected]);\n    } else {\n      expect(response).toEqual({ status: 400, body: \"invalid order\" });\n      expect(() => runBatch([order])).toThrow(\"invalid order\");\n    }\n  }\n});\n\ntest(\"batch preserves ordering and inputs and rejects an invalid later item\", () => {\n  const first = Object.freeze({ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 });\n  const second = Object.freeze({ quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 });\n  const input = [first, second, first];\n  expect(runBatch(input)).toEqual([250, 99, 250]);\n  expect(runBatch(input)).toEqual([250, 99, 250]);\n  expect(input).toEqual([first, second, first]);\n  expect(() => runBatch([first, { quantity: 0, unitPrice: 5 }])).toThrow(\"invalid order\");\n});\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/consumer.ts",
      "bytes": 240,
      "sha256": "aab57ed07de2879c106024b047013e177ac193a80ba5c73e7c6a3e3d7b539a98",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { runBatch, type Order } from \"./index\";\n\nexport function invoiceTotals(): Array<number | null> {\n  const orders: Order[] = [\n    { quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 },\n    { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 },\n  ];\n  return runBatch(orders);\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/adapters/http.ts",
      "bytes": 393,
      "sha256": "f005e4e3b583e113367a7a1335642273ba732b8d8f3e9d4342f3ef1471fee99f",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { isOrder, total } from \"../domain/order\";\n\nexport function handle(raw: string): { status: number; body: string } {\n  let order: unknown;\n  try {\n    order = JSON.parse(raw);\n  } catch {\n    return { status: 400, body: \"invalid json\" };\n  }\n  if (!isOrder(order)) return { status: 400, body: \"invalid order\" };\n  return { status: 200, body: JSON.stringify({ total: total(order) }) };\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/contract.test.mjs",
      "bytes": 761,
      "sha256": "b4c462080c43283cdc7b8ba9d10a9abccd9a7847c9c7950ea1cb06d56bfacfdc",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { test, expect } from 'bun:test';\nimport { handle, runBatch } from './index.ts';\nimport { invoiceTotals } from './consumer.ts';\ntest('stable HTTP success and error contract', () => {\n  expect(handle('{\"quantity\":2,\"unitPrice\":125}')).toEqual({ status: 200, body: '{\"total\":250}' });\n  expect(handle('{')).toEqual({ status: 400, body: 'invalid json' });\n  for (const raw of ['null', '{}', '{\"quantity\":0,\"unitPrice\":125}', '{\"quantity\":1,\"unitPrice\":-1}']) expect(handle(raw)).toEqual({ status: 400, body: 'invalid order' });\n});\ntest('private batch and consumer contracts', () => {\n  expect(runBatch([])).toEqual([]);\n  expect(invoiceTotals()).toEqual([250, 99]);\n  expect(() => runBatch([{ quantity: 0, unitPrice: 125 }])).toThrow('invalid order');\n});\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/.gitignore",

    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/batch.ts",
      "bytes": 308,
      "sha256": "32503b345c501e565db540660d2f5a00563bb8e63dfa4284c22bb049149cf367",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { isOrder, total, type Order } from \"./domain/order\";\n\nexport function runBatch(orders: Order[]): Array<number | null> {\n  return orders.map(order => {\n    if (!isOrder(order)) throw new Error(\"invalid order\");\n    const value = total(order);\n    return Number.isFinite(value) ? value : null;\n  });\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/tsconfig.json",

      "sha256": "1f03f1a88cd4309c2b648faa3c2a0a0495a06a77b00b742669bc8f52295d6c0e",

    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/working/domain/order.ts",
      "bytes": 683,
      "sha256": "50c164978fa97b21220ab7b0d56186c2fbc9d2adf5e40a9cfc6994dea6f89af3",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export type Order = { quantity: number; unitPrice: number };\n\n/** Numeric policy shared by the two entry boundaries. No transport decisions. */\nexport function isOrder(value: unknown): value is Order {\n  return typeof value === \"object\" && value !== null\n    && \"quantity\" in value && typeof value.quantity === \"number\"\n    && Number.isInteger(value.quantity) && value.quantity > 0\n    && \"unitPrice\" in value && typeof value.unitPrice === \"number\"\n    && Number.isInteger(value.unitPrice) && value.unitPrice >= 0;\n}\n\n/** Pure arithmetic for domain input already accepted at its boundary. */\nexport function total(order: Order): number {\n  return order.quantity * order.unitPrice;\n}\n"
    },

      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# The Question\nWhy must finite normalization belong to batch while domain arithmetic and HTTP stay unchanged?\n# The Code in Question\ninput/domain/order.ts:1-15 and input/target.md:2-3.\n# What We Found\n[Direct] input/target.md:3 assigns finite output policy to batch and requires nullable callers, direct domain calls, unchanged total:number and HTTP. [Direct] input/domain/order.ts:3,12 separates validation from transport and defines total as pure arithmetic. [Supported] outputs/why-findings.md records one fixture commit, no remote or PR identifiers and no incident rationale.\n# What We Can Reasonably Infer\nExplicit batch normalization appears intended to preserve legacy results without serialization coupling, given both constraints in input/target.md:3. Original intent behind legacy serialization remains unknown.\n# What We Don't Know\nWhether legacy null was intentional is unknown. Local history/blame/follow and incident-message searches supplied no earlier rationale. One multi-path blame failed and one corrected retry succeeded. External categories were not searched; this synthetic fixture has no associated external provenance IDs. Exposed connector capabilities are not being called unavailable.\n# Sources Consulted\n- Source control: one fixture commit and local history/blame, no remote or PR IDs.\n- Issue tracker: not searched; no fixture provenance IDs or leads.\n- Long-form documents: target.md and investigator findings; external docs not searched, no fixture leads.\n- Team chat: not searched; no fixture provenance leads.\n- Infrastructure observability: not searched; no deployment or incident IDs.\n- Error tracking: not searched; no event/release IDs.\n- Analytics: not searched; no associated product or dataset IDs.\n- Code comments: domain/order.ts:3,12 verified.\n# Confidence Summary\nCurrent requirements are direct; original historical motivation is unknown.\nPreserve domain numeric type, HTTP, ordinary totals, invalid errors and overflow null. Change batch finite policy and caller types. Avoid HTTP/JSON coupling and invented motivation. Risk: number[] hides null and domain normalization violates ownership.\n\nCaptured with light compression from read-only native why synthesizer /root/why_synthesis, assignment why-synthesis-1, SHA 54c6a8f432d247f165933d990ef8654874c6a39a, actual model unknown. Method {\"sampleCount\":1,\"sampleDefinition\":\"synthesize local history findings\",\"order\":\"evidence then confidence then constraints\"}.\n"

      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/candidate-a/plan.md",
      "bytes": 485,
      "sha256": "e5d44d293ea3413f34f751eb127dfb734e302ef7ffd59f5eda6acb619d34395e",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Candidate phases\n1. Ground from exact frozen architect sources, supplied application and parent how/why.\n2. Sketch function-local policy, compare collection alternative and screen eight flags.\n3. Return design-only result for coordinator synthesis.\n4. Implementation remains coordinator-owned.\n5. Scrap and reground if synthesis or implementation exposes repeated ownership deviations.\n\nNo task-list tool is exposed to this worker. This durable list records the same phase sequence.\n"

    {
      "path": "concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/candidate-a/design.md",
      "bytes": 10466,
      "sha256": "b83b58e1476ef0f8b0424c1cb7a46228ba988e7fbbe92b2b2493617f69ed4830",
      "owner": "local-013-repair-03-boundary-design",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Candidate a design\n\n## Problem\n\nThe committed batch returns Infinity for accepted finite integer operands whose product overflows. The immutable legacy reference returns null because its HTTP JSON round trip converts Infinity to null. Its number[] annotation was inaccurate. The new target explicitly requires Array<number|null> for runBatch and invoiceTotals while total(order):number stays pure and HTTP stays unchanged. I read every application file, the literal regression and reference, and the coordinator's how/why artifacts. The observed literal red record has two failing overflow positions. Historical motivation for the old transport coupling remains unknown.\n\n## Usage (caller's view)\n\nConsumers import the existing entry points. Batch returns one total per valid input in order, with null for a nonfinite computed total. Invalid orders throw invalid order. Callers must handle null explicitly.\n\n~~~ts\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from \"./index\";\nconst ordinary: Array<number | null> = runBatch([{ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 }]);\n// [250]\nconst overflow: Array<number | null> = runBatch([{ quantity: 1e308, unitPrice: 2 }]);\n// [null]\nconst orders: Order[] = [{ quantity: 0, unitPrice: 2 }];\n// runBatch(orders) throws Error(\"invalid order\").\n~~~\n\nThe existing application consumer keeps the same invocation and updates its return annotation.\n\n~~~ts\nimport { runBatch, type Order } from \"./index\";\nexport function invoiceTotals(): Array<number | null> {\n  const orders: Order[] = [{ quantity: 2, unitPrice: 125 }, { quantity: 1, unitPrice: 99 }];\n  return runBatch(orders);\n}\n~~~\n\nHTTP callers keep their existing wire contract.\n\n~~~ts\nimport { handle } from \"./index\";\nconst response = handle('{\"quantity\":1e308,\"unitPrice\":2}');\n// { status: 200, body: '{\"total\":null}' }\n~~~\n\n## Shape\n\nThe result data structure is an invocation-local mapped Array<number|null>. There is no shared mutable collection, cache, configurable conversion policy or new public export. The callback owns its numeric total until it returns either that finite value or null. Each call builds a fresh result and does not mutate its inputs, per separate-before-serializing-shared-state.\n\n~~~ts\n// domain/order.ts, unchanged existing definitions\nexport type Order = { quantity: number; unitPrice: number };\nexport function isOrder(value: unknown): value is Order {\n  throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n}\nexport function total(order: Order): number {\n  throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n}\n\n// batch.ts, new result annotation and local policy\nimport { isOrder, total, type Order } from \"./domain/order\";\nexport function runBatch(orders: Order[]): Array<number | null> {\n  // TODO Map in order. Validate each order with isOrder or throw invalid order.\n  // TODO Call total(order) directly exactly once for the accepted order.\n  // TODO Return the numeric total when Number.isFinite(total), otherwise null.\n  throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n}\n\n// consumer.ts, same caller flow with an accurate return annotation\nexport function invoiceTotals(): Array<number | null> {\n  throw new Error(\"not implemented\");\n}\n~~~\n\nDomain owns order acceptance and pure multiplication. Batch owns its finite-output compatibility policy, per boundary-discipline. HTTP owns JSON parsing, status and serialization and needs no edit. index.ts retains its current exports. consumer.ts exposes the accurate nullable return. No legacy.ts enters the final tree. Existing source imports of domain helpers remain intentional domain reuse; the local finite decision is not exported or separately importable.\n\nBatch's single operation hides validation, direct arithmetic, ordering and finite normalization behind the existing runBatch call. Nullable output remains visible because it is real behavior that the caller must handle, per type-system-discipline. This interface is no larger than the task needs. Tracing batch needs batch.ts and domain/order.ts; the application consumer adds one file. The map needs no secondary index or deferred cache. Validation rejects nonfinite operands before arithmetic, while finite multiplication can still overflow. total's number type must permit that JavaScript arithmetic result.\n\nCalling runBatch twice returns equivalent fresh arrays. If a later order is invalid, the local partial map is discarded and the function throws; there is no persisted partial result to reconcile. A process crash leaves no shared state. There are no locks, recovery writes or externally visible transitions.\n\n## Synthesis decision\n\nThis is candidate a, proposed as the function-local base for the coordinator's later synthesis. No other runner output or parent rubric was read. Cross-candidate selection, grafts and rejections are pending the fresh judge and coordinator; this worker makes no claim that synthesis or implementation has occurred. The proposed base keeps all required behavior in the existing batch boundary and adds no public coordination steps.\n\n## Tradeoffs accepted\n\n- We accept a local finite policy next to validation in exchange for keeping the existing call and avoiding an extra collection lifecycle.\n- We accept Array<number|null> in caller signatures in exchange for matching the actual legacy overflow result.\n- We accept a runtime finiteness check in exchange for preserving domain total:number and all accepted finite integer inputs.\n- We accept allocation of a fresh output array on each call in exchange for invocation isolation and input immutability.\n\n## Alternatives considered\n\nA private BatchTotals collection could own result accumulation and exposure, with runBatch validating each order, calling total directly and handing each numeric result to a collection method. It hides finite normalization and prevents outside mutation of its private results. Its internal constructor, add and finish lifecycle must still be coordinated by runBatch, so the object creates more internal interface for this stateless map. Its depth would improve if the batch had additional invariants such as grouped aggregation or incremental input; neither exists here. This is a structurally distinct alternative with object-owned accumulation rather than callback-local mapped results.\n\nA shared domain nullable-total function would reduce a check at batch but move boundary compatibility into arithmetic and widen the domain return type. It hides the wrong policy from the wrong callers and violates the explicit target, so it is rejected.\n\n## Open questions and risks\n\n- Will native checks confirm both overflow positions, ordinary totals, invalid and nonfinite operands, unchanged HTTP responses and invocation isolation under this shape?\n- Will the strict compiler verify all callers expose null without casts or misleading number[] annotations?\n- Does future work introduce incremental batches or grouped state that would justify replacing the local map with a private collection, and should that future requirement trigger regrounding rather than speculative code now?\n\nThe first two questions require execution evidence rather than new human authorization. The current scope already authorizes the coordinator's implementation; this worker is design-only. No explicit checkpoint was requested.\n\n## Next implementation step\n\nAfter fresh judging and synthesis, update only the owned working copy's batch finite mapping and batch consumer annotations, then run literal green, compatibility checks, all-callers inspection, legacy-absence checks and the specified strict compiler through Bun.\n\n## Eight-flag screen\n\n| Flag | Finding and constraint |\n| --- | --- |\n| Shallow module | Existing runBatch hides validation, arithmetic orchestration and finite compatibility behind one call; no helper API adds coordination. |\n| Information leakage | HTTP wire types stay in the HTTP adapter; batch exposes a numeric nullable result, not JSON or response objects. |\n| Temporal decomposition | No load/validate/transform/save modules; batch owns one complete collection operation and domain owns order knowledge. |\n| Pass-through method | runBatch adds collection validation and finite policy; it does more than forwarding total. No new forwarding helper is added. |\n| Split ownership | Each invocation owns its new mapped array; no second writer, persistent accumulator or copied mutable state exists. |\n| Two ways to do one task | Batch calls domain total directly; the HTTP/JSON batch route and legacy module are absent from the final shape. |\n| Importable internals | The finite decision is callback-local and cannot be imported. Existing domain exports remain intentional shared domain operations. |\n| Hand-synced list | There is no new registry or duplicated list. Application sources and callers must be found from actual imports during verification. |\n\n## Workflow limits and deviation signals\n\nThis worker executed the exact frozen architect runner discipline inline because no direct installed skill invocation tool is exposed. Parent orchestration owns how/why workers, arena pair dispatch and fresh judging. This worker inspected their supplied grounding artifacts and independently read the target files; it did not execute those parent workers or tests. Worker identity is /root/candidate_a, requested model inherit-parent and actual model unknown; neither inheritance nor this receipt proves model diversity. No model rejection or alias resolution occurred in this assignment.\n\nIf implementation needs JSON in batch, a nullable domain return, shared result state or repeated casts, scrap this candidate and re-run how over the actual implementation, subtract the offending abstraction and re-explore two whole shapes. Two similar independent deviations signal an ownership error. Minor syntax details alone do not require redesign.\n\n## Exact result receipt\n\n~~~json\n{\n  \"assignmentId\": \"candidate-a\",\n  \"workerId\": \"/root/candidate_a\",\n  \"inputRevision\": \"54c6a8f432d247f165933d990ef8654874c6a39a\",\n  \"outputOwner\": \"/root/candidate_a\",\n  \"outputPath\": \"/Users/digital-hawk/Herd/pstack-workflow-013-20261016/concrete-repairs/boundary-design/outputs/candidate-a/design.md\",\n  \"method\": {\n    \"sampleCount\": 1,\n    \"sampleDefinition\": \"one whole subsystem design with caller-first types and eight-flag screen\",\n    \"order\": \"usage then shape then rationale then screen\"\n  },\n  \"requestedModel\": \"inherit-parent\",\n  \"actualModel\": \"unknown\",\n  \"requestedEffort\": \"inherit-parent\",\n  \"actualEffort\": \"unknown\",\n  \"retryCount\": 0,\n  \"status\": \"completed\"\n}\n~~~\n"
    },

Whole-shape distinction, ownership coherence and all eight red flags.\n3. Interface depth and short call chains, smallest useful public API.\n4.       "text": "# Fresh judge return\nNative worker /root/judge_1, assignment judge-1, inputRevision 54c6a8f432d247f165933d990ef8654874c6a39a. Requested model inherit-parent; actual unknown. Readonly return captured by coordinator. Method {\"sampleCount\":2,\"sampleDefinition\":\"two completed whole-shape candidates against private rubric\",\"order\":\"inspect both then compare then recommend\"}. No case grade.\n\nFresh judge recommendation: select candidate A's function-local map as the base. Candidate B is structurally distinct and compatible in design, but its private collection adds an internal lifecycle and a second array allocation without protecting additional required state.\n\nBoth completed candidate artifacts were read end to end after coordinator inspection. The judge also inspected private rubric, target, actual batch/domain/HTTP/consumer/export sources, pristine reference, literal regression, compatibility tests, grounding, frozen rationale and red flags.\n\n| Criterion | A | B |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Behavioral/type compatibility | Direct total, finite normalization, nullable batch/consumer, unchanged domain/HTTP. Covers both overflow positions and invalid errors. | Same contracts; runBatch calls total before private collection normalization. |\n| Whole shape and ownership | Callback-local normalization, map owns array construction. Real structural distinction. |\n| Depth and tracing | One public call hides full operation, batch and domain only, no extra internal protocol. | Equally small public call, but constructor/append/snapshot hide little added complexity. Copy unnecessary after accumulator becomes unreachable. |\n| Caller-first and rationale | Usage precedes types, examples agree, every template section present, synthesis explicitly deferred. | Usage precedes types, examples agree, every section present, synthesis deferred. |\n| Implementation/verification feasibility | Local variable, finite check and two annotations fit without casts or transport/domain changes. | Feasible but class/methods/copy lack a concrete need. |\n\nSource premise confirmed: raw total and number[] in input/batch.ts and consumer.ts; pure multiplication in domain; HTTP serializes overflow; reference batch returns null despite its inaccurate annotation. Graft B's complete ordered array or exception explanation, and distinction between public depth and internal collection overhead. These improve rationale without adding collection code.\n\nReject B's class because A provides the same capability and isolation without constructor/append/snapshot coordination.       "text": "import {readdir} from 'node:fs/promises';\nimport {event,snapshot} from './control.mjs';\nconst files=[];async function walk(p){for(const d of await readdir(p,{withFileTypes:true})){const f=p+'/'+d.name;if(d.isDirectory())await walk(f);else files.push(f);}}await walk('outputs/working');\nconst app=files.filter(f=>f.endsWith('.ts'));\nconst callers=[];const changes=[];\nfor(const f of files){const input=f.replace('outputs/working/','input/');if(await Bun.file(input).exists()){const a=await Bun.file(f).bytes(),b=await Bun.file(input).bytes();if(Bun.CryptoHasher.hash('sha256',a,'hex')!==Bun.CryptoHasher.hash('sha256',b,'hex'))changes.push(f);}}\nfor(const f of app){const text=await Bun.file(f).text();if(/\\brunBatch\\s*\\(/.test(text))callers.push({file:f,source:text});}\nconst batch=await Bun.file('outputs/working/batch.ts').text();\nconst now=await snapshot();const before=await Bun.file('outputs/immutable-before.json').json();\nconst checks={immutableSources:JSON.stringify(now)===JSON.stringify(before),domainUnchanged:!changes.includes('outputs/working/domain/order.ts'),httpUnchanged:!changes.includes('outputs/working/adapters/http.ts'),onlyAuthorizedAppChanges:changes.every(f=>['outputs/working/batch.ts','outputs/working/consumer.ts'].includes(f)),legacyAbsent:!files.some(f=>/legacy/i.test(f)),batchDirectDomain:/import\\s*\\{[^}]*\\btotal\\b[^}]*\\}\\s*from\\s*[\"']\\.\\/domain\\/order[\"']/.test(batch)&&/\\btotal\\(order\\)/.test(batch),batchNoTransport:!(/\\bJSON\\b|adapters\\/http|\\bhandle\\s*\\(/.test(batch))};\nconst result={checks,changes,callers,files,note:'Source inspection supports architectural constraints; runtime behavior is established separately by Bun test results.'};\nawait Bun.write('outputs/source-inspection.json',JSON.stringify(result,null,2));await Bun.write('outputs/immutable-after.json',JSON.stringify(now,null,2));console.log(JSON.stringify(result,null,2));if(!Object.values(checks).every(Boolean))process.exit(1);\n"
